  TN1248; 45 pages. This is the source for item 3 (SPI master mode, SPI_SS_B

  from external flash.

       1 +"""Merge KiCad 10 per-symbol .kicad_symdir libraries into singl
         +e .kicad_sym files.                                            
       2 +                                                               
       3 +SKiDL's KiCad loader reads single-file symbol libraries only, s
         +o the libraries this                                           
       4 +design uses are flattened into ./lib with their original names 
         +(Device, Analog_ADC...).                                       
       5 +"""                                                            
       6 +import os, re, sys                                             
       7 +                                                               
       8 +SRC = "/home/devb/bin/kicad10-root/share/kicad/symbols"        
       9 +DST = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "lib")     
      10 +LIBS = """Device Analog_ADC FPGA_Lattice Memory_RAM Memory_Flas
         +h Memory_EEPROM Interface_USB                                  
      11 +Amplifier_Operational Amplifier_Difference Regulator_Linear Reg
         +ulator_Switching                                               
      12 +Regulator_SwitchedCapacitor Power_Protection Diode Oscillator C
         +onnector Connector_Generic                                     
      13 +Mechanical power""".split()                                    
      14 +                                                               
      15 +def body(text):                                                
      16 +    # Strip the outer (kicad_symbol_lib ...) wrapper and its he
         +ader fields; keep symbol blocks.                               
      17 +    i = text.index("(symbol ")                                 
      18 +    j = text.rstrip().rindex(")")                              
      19 +    return text[i:j].rstrip()                                  
      20 +                                                               
      21 +for lib in LIBS:                                               
      22 +    d = os.path.join(SRC, lib + ".kicad_symdir")               
      23 +    files = sorted(f for f in os.listdir(d) if f.endswith(".kic
         +ad_sym"))                                                      
      24 +    head = open(os.path.join(d, files[0])).read()              
      25 +    hdr = head[: head.index("(symbol ")].rstrip()              
      26 +    out = [hdr]                                                
      27 +    for f in files:                                            
      28 +        out.append("\t" + body(open(os.path.join(d, f)).read())
         +)                                                              
      29 +    out.append(")\n")                                          
      30 +    with open(os.path.join(DST, lib + ".kicad_sym"), "w") as fh
         +:                                                              
      31 +        fh.write("\n".join(out))                               
      32 +    print(lib, len(files))

       9 +def patch(pro):                                                
      10 +    d = json.load(open(pro))                                   
      11 +    ns = d["net_settings"]                                     
      12 +    base = dict(ns["classes"][0])                              
      13 +    pw = dict(base, name="Power", track_width=0.3, clearance=0.
         +15, priority=0)                                                
      14 +    ns["classes"] = [c for c in ns["classes"] if c["name"] != "
         +Power"] + [pw]

         + n} for n in POWER]

      38 +def resistor_mpn(v, tol, fp):                                  
      39 +    """Yageo thick-film (1%) / thin-film (0.1%) part number for
         + a resistor value."""                                          
      40 +    code = v.upper().replace("R", "")                          
      41 +    m = re.match(r"^([0-9.]+)([KM]?)$", code)                  
      42 +    mant, mult = m.group(1), m.group(2)                        
      43 +    mant = mant.replace(".", mult or "R") if "." in mant else m
         +ant + (mult or "R")                                            
      44 +    size = "1206" if "1206" in fp else "0603"                  
      45 +    return (f"RT{size}BRD07{mant}L" if tol == "0.1%" else f"RC{
         +size}FR-07{mant}L")                                            
      46 +                                                               
      47 +                                                               
      48 +# Murata part numbers (generic suggestions; check voltage ratin
         +g/derating at order time).                                     
      49 +CAP_MPN = {                                                    
      50 +    ("100nF", "0603"): "GRM188R71C104KA01D",                   
      51 +    ("1uF", "0603"): "GRM188R71C105KA12D",                     
      52 +    ("2.2uF", "0603"): "GRM188R61C225KE15D",                   
      53 +    ("2.2uF", "0805"): "GRM21BR71C225KA12L",                   
      54 +    ("4.7uF", "0805"): "GRM21BR71C475KE51L",                   
      55 +    ("10uF", "0805"): "GRM21BR61C106KE15L",                    
      56 +    ("22uF", "0805"): "GRM21BR61A226ME44L",                    
      57 +    ("27pF", "0603"): "GRM1885C1H270JA01D",                    
      58 +    ("22pF", "0603"): "GRM1885C1H220JA01D",                    
      59 +    ("10pF", "0603"): "GRM1885C1H100JA01D",                    
      60 +    ("330pF", "0603"): "GRM1885C1H331JA01D",                   
      61 +    ("6.8pF", "0603"): "GRM1885C1H6R8DA01D",                   
      62 +    ("15pF", "1206"): "GRM3195C2A150JA01D",                    
      63 +    ("2.2pF", "1206"): "GRM3195C2A2R2CA01D",

       1 +"""Compare connectivity of two KiCad netlists (net = set of ref
         +.pin). Prints differences."""                                  
       2 +import re                                                      
       3 +import sys                                                     
       4 +                                                               
       5 +                                                               
       6 +def load(path):                                                
       7 +    t = open(path).read()                                      
       8 +    nets = {}                                                  
       9 +    for m in re.finditer(r'\(net \(code "?\d+"?\) \(name "((?:\
         +\.|[^"])*)"\)(.*?)\)\s*(?=\(net |\)\s*\)\s*$)', t, re.S):      
      10 +        name, body = m.group(1), m.group(2)                    
      11 +        nodes = frozenset(f"{r}.{p}" for r, p in re.findall(r'\
         +(node \(ref "([^"]+)"\) \(pin "([^"]+)"\)', body))             
      12 +        nets[name] = nodes                                     
      13 +    return nets                                                
      14 +                                                               
      15 +                                                               
      16 +def main(a, b):                                                
      17 +    na, nb = load(a), load(b)                                  
      18 +    sa = {v: k for k, v in na.items() if not k.startswith("unco
         +nnected") and len(v) > 1}                                      
      19 +    sb = {v: k for k, v in nb.items() if not k.startswith("unco
         +nnected") and len(v) > 1}                                      
      20 +    only_a = [sa[s] for s in sa if s not in sb]                
      21 +    only_b = [sb[s] for s in sb if s not in sa]                
      22 +    print(f"{a}: {len(sa)} multi-pin nets; {b}: {len(sb)}")    
      23 +    # map pin -> net for diagnostics                           
      24 +    pa = {p: k for k, v in na.items() for p in v}              
      25 +    pb = {p: k for k, v in nb.items() for p in v}              
      26 +    for n in sorted(only_a):                                   
      27 +        pins = na[n]                                           
      28 +        others = sorted({pb.get(p, "<none>") for p in pins})   
      29 +        print(f"A-only {n} ({len(pins)} pins) -> in B as {other
         +s[:6]}")                                                       
      30 +    for n in sorted(only_b):                                   
      31 +        pins = nb[n]                                           
      32 +        others = sorted({pa.get(p, "<none>") for p in pins})   
      33 +        print(f"B-only {n} ({len(pins)} pins) <- from A {others
         +[:6]}")                                                        
      34 +                                                               
      35 +                                                               
      36 +if __name__ == "__main__":                                     
      37 +    main(sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2])

         +xt)

  the design, so fixing that tool is out of scope. Instead, I'll write a

● Found the issue: Schematizer used KiCad 7 format with single-level instance
  paths (/uuid), causing child-sheet contents to be attributed to the root. I'll
  write my own generator using KiCad 9/10 syntax, referencing a hierarchical
  KiCad 10 demo for the correct format.

● Bash(cd /home/devb/projects/AI/ai-circuit-design-tests/kicad-happy-tests/dual-
      adc-usb-2; cat > scripts/gen_pcf.py <<'EOF'
      """Generate an iCE40 .pcf pin-constraint fi…)
  ⎿  102 signals -> gateware/dual_adc_usb.pcf
     # iCE40HX4K-TQ144 pin constraints for dual_adc_usb (generated by scrip
     ts/gen_pcf.py
     … +18 lines (ctrl+o to expand)
  ⎿  Created scripts/gen_pcf.py (+27 -0)
       1 +"""Generate an iCE40 .pcf pin-constraint file from the schemati
         +c netlist (FPGA = ICE40HX4K-TQ144).                            
       2 +usage: python3 gen_pcf.py kicad/dual_adc_usb.net gateware/dual_
         +adc_usb.pcf"""                                                 
       3 +import os                                                      
       4 +import re                                                      
       5 +import sys                                                     
       6 +                                                               
       7 +sys.path.insert(0, os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))) 
       8 +from build_pcb_netlist import parse_netlist  # noqa: E402      
       9 +                                                               
      10 +comps, nets = parse_netlist(sys.argv[1])                       
      11 +fpga = [r for r, c in comps.items() if c["value"].startswith("I
         +CE40")][0]                                                     
      12 +SKIP = re.compile(r"^(GND|\+|-|VCCPLL|GNDPLL|unconnected|CFG_CD
         +ONE|CFG_CRESET)|/VCCPLL|/GNDPLL")                              
      13 +rows = []                                                      
      14 +for name, nodes in nets.items():                               
      15 +    for ref, pin in nodes:                                     
      16 +        if ref == fpga and not SKIP.search(name):              
      17 +            sig = name.split("/")[-1]                          
      18 +            m = re.match(r"^(DA|DB|SD_DQ|SD_A|SD_BA|FT_D|FT_ACB
         +US|GPIO)(\d+)$", sig)                                          
      19 +            sig = f"{m.group(1).lower()}[{m.group(2)}]" if m el
         +se sig.lower()                                                 
      20 +            rows.append((sig, int(pin)))                       
      21 +os.makedirs(os.path.dirname(sys.argv[2]), exist_ok=True)       
      22 +with open(sys.argv[2], "w") as f:                              
      23 +    f.write("# iCE40HX4K-TQ144 pin constraints for dual_adc_usb
         + (generated by scripts/gen_pcf.py\n")                          
      24 +    f.write("# from kicad/dual_adc_usb.net; do not edit by hand
         + -- FPGA pins follow the PCB).\n")                             
      25 +    for sig, pin in sorted(rows, key=lambda r: (re.sub(r"\[\d+\
         +]", "", r[0]), int(re.search(r"\d+", r[0][::-1][:0] + (re.finda
         +ll(r'\[(\d+)\]', r[0]) or ['0'])[0]).group()), r[1])):         
      26 +        f.write(f"set_io {sig} {pin}\n")

       91    - KiCad 10 ERC: **0 violations**.

       93  
       94 +### D12. Layout decisions made during routing                 
       95 +- **Inner layers are planes only.**                           
       96 +  - Option: let Freerouting use In1/In2 for signals. The first
          + run did, and it cut the planes into islands.                 
       97 +  - Chosen: mark In1/In2 as `power` in the DSN, so signals go 
          +on F/B only.                                                  
       98 +- **FPGA pin swapping.**                                      
       99 +  - Default bank-ordered assignment: the DA/DB/SDRAM/FIFO buse
          +s crossed over themselves, and routing stalled.               
      100 +  - Chosen: `scripts/pin_swap.py` orders FPGA pins by angle ar
          +ound the package to match the partner pads. FPGA pins are just
          + gateware constraints, so this is free.                       
      101 +- **ADC bus spacing.**                                        
      102 +  - 0.15/0.15 mm: the QFN-64 top/bottom rows couldn't escape (
          +2–5 links left on every attempt, including rip-up).           
      103 +  - 6 layers: more cost.                                      
      104 +  - Chosen: **0.1/0.1 mm net class for DA*/DB*/OF* only.** Thi
          +s needs an advanced process tier.                             
      105 +- **USB.**                                                    
      106 +  - Original orientation: D+/D− were 34–39 mm long with up to 
          +3 vias.                                                       
      107 +  - Chosen: rotate the FT2232H 180° so DM/DP face J1, put the 
          +USBLC6 in flow-through between them, and pre-route the pair at
          + 0.2 mm (about 10 mm each).                                   
      108 +- **Decoupling of the large ICs.** Decoupling capacitors for t
          +he FPGA, SDRAM and FT2232H are on B.Cu under the pin rows, so 
          +assembly is double-sided. There are fiducials on both sides.

● I've built the board: schematic, a fully routed 4-layer PCB, and fab files.
  The final checks are clean:
  - KiCad ERC: 0 violations.
  - DRC: 0 errors, 0 unrouted connections, 0 schematic/PCB mismatches. The 19
    warnings are 18 silkscreen nits plus one footprint-library copy note.
  - Netlists: the netlist exported from the schematic matches the SKiDL source
    exactly (172/172 multi-pin nets).

  It isn't ready to order until two parts are checked against their datasheets,
  which I couldn't download here (see below).

  Design choices (each with its options listed in design_decisions.md):

  ┌─────────┬────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │  Need   │                             Choice                             │
  ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │         │ BNC jacks, 1 MΩ ∥ ≈20 pF so scope probes compensate. A /10     │
  │ Inputs  │ divider, an OPA810 buffer and a THS4521 differential driver    │
  │         │ give ±10.6 V full scale.                                       │
  ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ ADC     │ LTC2291, dual 12-bit, run at 10 MSPS from its own 10 MHz       │
  │         │ oscillator. It's a 25 MSPS part, so there's headroom.          │
  ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │         │ iCE40HX4K FPGA with 8 MB SDRAM: 0.2 s of both channels at full │
  │ Capture │  rate (0.1 s required). The board captures to memory and       │
  │         │ uploads afterwards, because streaming 40 MB/s over USB 2.0 is  │
  │         │ marginal.                                                      │
  ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │         │ FT2232H on USB 2.0 High-Speed. One channel moves data (about 8 │
  │ USB     │  MB/s, so a 4 MB capture takes about 0.5 s); the other         │
  │         │ reprograms the FPGA's flash with iceprog.                      │
  ├─────────┼────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │         │ Estimated ≈280 mA at 5 V against a 500 mA budget, so a USB-B   │
  │ Power   │ connector is enough and USB-C wasn't needed. That estimate     │
  │         │ assumes the two unverified parts draw about what I expect.     │
  └─────────┴────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Things you should know:
  - Datasheets not checked: analog.com timed out and ftdichip.com returned 403.
    So the pin functions, reference/bypass network and configuration for the
    LTC2291 and FT2232H come from the KiCad symbols plus memory. The TI and
    Lattice parts were checked against their PDFs.
  - Fab tier: the ADC data bus uses 0.1 mm tracks and 0.1 mm spacing. At 0.15 mm
    it wouldn't route on two signal layers. That needs an advanced fab tier;
    JLCPCB 4-layer supports it.
  - Anti-aliasing is weak: the filter is only 2nd order (about 4–5 MHz). The
    cheapest fix is to clock the ADC at 20 MHz and decimate in the FPGA.
  - EMC risk is high per kicad-happy (score 1/100). It's mostly structural: only
    two signal layers, so signals switch reference planes at every via. A
    6-layer board is the real fix.
  - Your schematic generator failed: SKiDL's generate_schematic(tool=KICAD10),
    via schematizer, produced a schematic that merged GND with +3V3 and dropped
    many pins (910 ERC violations). It also writes KiCad 7-format files with
    single-level instance paths on the sub-sheets. I replaced it with a
    label-based writer (scripts/sch_writer.py); the result reads more like a
    netlist with symbols than a signal-flow drawing. The bad output is kept in
    schematizer_out/ if you want to debug it.
  - Analyzer false positives: kicad-happy reports some errors that I ruled out;
    the reasoning is in the review. One example is LDO overheating, which
    assumed near-maximum currents.
  - Not done: the FPGA gateware and host software. There are no test points, and
    parts lifecycle wasn't checked (no distributor API keys). Nothing has been
    committed to git.

  Re-running Freerouting gives slightly different results, so the board in
  kicad/ is the verified one. I can publish the design review as a shareable
  page if you want to pass it along.

  Files are in dual-adc-usb-2/:
  - README.md
  - design_decisions.md
  - design_review.md
  - dual_adc_usb.py (the SKiDL source)
  - kicad/ (schematic, board, schematic PDF)
  - fab/ (Gerbers, drill, BOM, placement file)
  - gateware/dual_adc_usb.pcf (FPGA pin constraints)

      1 # Executive Summary: dual-adc-usb-2

● I've written dual-adc-usb-2/executive_summary.md, about one page long. It
  covers:
  - The task: the requirements from the prompt and the tools used.
  - The outcome: a table of the check results (ERC, DRC, schematic/PCB match,
    netlist match, BOM, SPICE). It ends with the status: a buildable prototype
    that isn't cleared to order yet.
  - The design: a table mapping each requirement to how the board meets it,
    including why USB-C wasn't needed.
  - How it was done: the seven steps, including the failed SKiDL schematic
    generator and its replacement.
  - Risks and open items:
    - the two datasheets I couldn't get (LTC2291 and FT2232H);
    - the 0.1 mm fab rule on the ADC bus;
    - the weak anti-alias filter;
    - EMC risk;
    - the USB lines not being impedance-controlled;
    - work not done, such as the FPGA gateware and host software.
